<figcaption><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_table"><span id="A7.T16.3.1.1" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">Table 16</span>: </span><span id="A7.T16.4.2" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">Example prompt demonstrating GPT-4’s visual input capability. The prompt requires image understanding.</span></figcaption>
</figure>
<figure id="A7.T17" class="ltx_table">
<table id="A7.T17.3" class="ltx_tabular ltx_align_middle">
<colgroup>
<col style="width: 50%" />
<col style="width: 50%" />
</colgroup>
<tbody>
<tr id="A7.T17.3.4" class="odd ltx_tr">
<td colspan="2" id="A7.T17.3.4.1" class="ltx_td ltx_align_justify ltx_align_top ltx_border_tt"><span id="A7.T17.3.4.1.1" class="ltx_inline-block ltx_align_top"> <span id="A7.T17.3.4.1.1.1" class="ltx_p" style="width:469.8pt;"> <span id="A7.T17.3.4.1.1.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">GPT-4 visual input example, Pixel to Paper Summaries</span>:</span> </span></td>
</tr>
<tr id="A7.T17.3.3" class="even ltx_tr">
<td id="A7.T17.3.3.4" class="ltx_td ltx_align_justify ltx_align_top ltx_border_t"><span id="A7.T17.3.3.4.1" class="ltx_inline-block ltx_align_top"> <span id="A7.T17.3.3.4.1.1" class="ltx_p" style="width:36.1pt;">User</span> </span></td>
<td id="A7.T17.3.3.3" class="ltx_td ltx_align_justify ltx_align_top ltx_border_t"><span id="A7.T17.3.3.3.3" class="ltx_inline-block ltx_align_top"> <span id="A7.T17.3.3.3.3.3" class="ltx_p" style="width:361.3pt;">Below is part of the InstuctGPT paper. Could you read and summarize it to me?<br />
<br />
<span id="A7.T17.3.3.3.3.3.3" class="ltx_tabular ltx_align_middle"> <span id="A7.T17.3.3.3.3.3.3.3" class="ltx_tr"> <span id="A7.T17.3.3.3.3.3.3.3.3" class="ltx_td ltx_align_center"><img src="/html/2303.08774/assets/assets/instruct-gpt-1.jpeg" id="A7.T17.1.1.1.1.1.1.1.1.g1" class="ltx_graphics ltx_img_portrait" width="180" height="233" alt="[Uncaptioned image]" /> <img src="/html/2303.08774/assets/assets/instruct-gpt-2.jpeg" id="A7.T17.2.2.2.2.2.2.2.2.g2" class="ltx_graphics ltx_img_portrait" width="180" height="233" alt="[Uncaptioned image]" /> <img src="/html/2303.08774/assets/assets/instruct-gpt-3.jpeg" id="A7.T17.3.3.3.3.3.3.3.3.g3" class="ltx_graphics ltx_img_portrait" width="180" height="233" alt="[Uncaptioned image]" /><br />
</span></span> </span></span> </span></td>
</tr>
<tr id="A7.T17.3.5" class="odd ltx_tr">
<td id="A7.T17.3.5.1" class="ltx_td ltx_align_justify ltx_align_top"><span id="A7.T17.3.5.1.1" class="ltx_inline-block ltx_align_top"> <span id="A7.T17.3.5.1.1.1" class="ltx_p" style="width:36.1pt;">GPT-4</span> </span></td>
<td id="A7.T17.3.5.2" class="ltx_td ltx_align_justify ltx_align_top"><span id="A7.T17.3.5.2.1" class="ltx_inline-block ltx_align_top"> <span id="A7.T17.3.5.2.1.1" class="ltx_p" style="width:361.3pt;">The InstructGPT paper focuses on training large language models to follow instructions with human feedback. The authors note that making language models larger doesn’t inherently make them better at following a user’s intent. Large models can generate outputs that are untruthful, toxic, or simply unhelpful.<br />
<br />
To address this issue, the authors fine-tune language models on a wide range of tasks using human feedback. They start with a set of labeler-written prompts and responses, then collect a dataset of labeler demonstrations of the desired model behavior. They fine-tune GPT-3 using supervised learning and then use reinforcement learning from human feedback to further fine-tune the model. The resulting model, called InstructGPT, shows improvements in truthfulness and reductions in toxic output generation while having minimal performance regressions on public NLP datasets.<br />
<br />
The authors conclude that fine-tuning with human feedback is a promising direction for aligning language models with human intent.<br />
</span> </span></td>
</tr>
<tr id="A7.T17.3.6" class="even ltx_tr">
<td id="A7.T17.3.6.1" class="ltx_td ltx_align_justify ltx_align_top"><span id="A7.T17.3.6.1.1" class="ltx_inline-block ltx_align_top"> <span id="A7.T17.3.6.1.1.1" class="ltx_p" style="width:36.1pt;">User</span> </span></td>
<td id="A7.T17.3.6.2" class="ltx_td ltx_align_justify ltx_align_top"><span id="A7.T17.3.6.2.1" class="ltx_inline-block ltx_align_top"> <span id="A7.T17.3.6.2.1.1" class="ltx_p" style="width:361.3pt;">Could you explain the process described in Figure 2?<br />
</span> </span></td>
</tr>
<tr id="A7.T17.3.7" class="odd ltx_tr">
<td id="A7.T17.3.7.1" class="ltx_td ltx_align_justify ltx_align_top ltx_border_bb"><span id="A7.T17.3.7.1.1" class="ltx_inline-block ltx_align_top"> <span id="A7.T17.3.7.1.1.1" class="ltx_p" style="width:36.1pt;">GPT-4</span> </span></td>
<td id="A7.T17.3.7.2" class="ltx_td ltx_align_justify ltx_align_top ltx_border_bb"><span id="A7.T17.3.7.2.1" class="ltx_inline-block ltx_align_top"> <span id="A7.T17.3.7.2.1.1" class="ltx_p" style="width:361.3pt;">Figure 2 in the InstructGPT paper illustrates the three steps of their method:<br />
<br />
1. Supervised fine-tuning (SFT): They collect demonstration data, which consists of labeler-written prompts and desired output behavior. This data is used to fine-tune GPT-3 with supervised learning.<br />
<br />
2. Reward model (RM) training: They collect comparison data, where a labeler ranks the outputs from several model samples (from best to worst). This data is used to train the reward model.<br />
<br />
3. Reinforcement learning using Proximal Policy Optimization (PPO): They optimize a policy against the reward model using reinforcement learning. For each new prompt sampled from the dataset, the policy generates an output. The reward model calculates a reward for the output, and the reward is used to update the policy using the PPO algorithm.<br />
<br />
These three steps are iteratively performed to train the InstructGPT model, which aims to generate outputs that align better with human preferences and follow instructions more effectively.</span> </span></td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<figcaption><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_table"><span id="A7.T17.5.1.1" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">Table 17</span>: </span><span id="A7.T17.6.2" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">Example prompt demonstrating GPT-4’s visual input capability.</span></figcaption>
</figure>
<figure id="A7.T18" class="ltx_table">
<table id="A7.T18.1" class="ltx_tabular ltx_align_middle">
<colgroup>
<col style="width: 50%" />
<col style="width: 50%" />
</colgroup>
<tbody>
<tr id="A7.T18.1.2" class="odd ltx_tr">
<td colspan="2" id="A7.T18.1.2.1" class="ltx_td ltx_align_justify ltx_align_top ltx_border_tt"><span id="A7.T18.1.2.1.1" class="ltx_inline-block ltx_align_top"> <span id="A7.T18.1.2.1.1.1" class="ltx_p" style="width:361.3pt;"> <span id="A7.T18.1.2.1.1.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">GPT-4 visual input example, Chicken Nugget Map</span>:</span> </span></td>
</tr>
<tr id="A7.T18.1.1" class="even ltx_tr">
<td id="A7.T18.1.1.2" class="ltx_td ltx_align_justify ltx_align_top ltx_border_t"><span id="A7.T18.1.1.2.1" class="ltx_inline-block ltx_align_top"> <span id="A7.T18.1.1.2.1.1" class="ltx_p" style="width:36.1pt;">User</span> </span></td>
<td id="A7.T18.1.1.1" class="ltx_td ltx_align_justify ltx_align_top ltx_border_t"><span id="A7.T18.1.1.1.1" class="ltx_inline-block ltx_align_top"> <span id="A7.T18.1.1.1.1.1" class="ltx_p" style="width:325.2pt;">Can you explain this meme?<br />
<br />
<img src="/html/2303.08774/assets/assets/nuggets.png" id="A7.T18.1.1.1.1.1.g1" class="ltx_graphics ltx_img_square" width="479" height="566" alt="[Uncaptioned image]" /><br />
</span> </span></td>
</tr>
<tr id="A7.T18.1.3" class="odd ltx_tr">
<td id="A7.T18.1.3.1" class="ltx_td ltx_align_justify ltx_align_top ltx_border_bb"><span id="A7.T18.1.3.1.1" class="ltx_inline-block ltx_align_top"> <span id="A7.T18.1.3.1.1.1" class="ltx_p" style="width:36.1pt;">GPT-4</span> </span></td>
<td id="A7.T18.1.3.2" class="ltx_td ltx_align_justify ltx_align_top ltx_border_bb"><span id="A7.T18.1.3.2.1" class="ltx_inline-block ltx_align_top"> <span id="A7.T18.1.3.2.1.1" class="ltx_p" style="width:325.2pt;">This meme is a joke that combines two unrelated things: pictures of the earth from space and chicken nuggets.<br />
The text of the meme suggests that the image below is a beautiful picture of the earth from space. However, the image is actually of chicken nuggets arranged to vaguely resemble a map of the world.<br />
The humor in this meme comes from the unexpected juxtaposition of the text and the image. The text sets up an expectation of a majestic image of the earth, but the image is actually something mundane and silly.</span> </span></td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<figcaption><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_table"><span id="A7.T18.3.1.1" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">Table 18</span>: </span><span id="A7.T18.4.2" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">Example prompt demonstrating GPT-4’s visual input capability.</span></figcaption>
</figure>
<figure id="A7.T19" class="ltx_table">
<table id="A7.T19.1" class="ltx_tabular ltx_align_middle">
<colgroup>
<col style="width: 50%" />
<col style="width: 50%" />
</colgroup>
<tbody>
<tr id="A7.T19.1.2" class="odd ltx_tr">
<td colspan="2" id="A7.T19.1.2.1" class="ltx_td ltx_align_justify ltx_align_top ltx_border_tt"><span id="A7.T19.1.2.1.1" class="ltx_inline-block ltx_align_top"> <span id="A7.T19.1.2.1.1.1" class="ltx_p" style="width:361.3pt;"> <span id="A7.T19.1.2.1.1.1.1" class="ltx_text ltx_font_bold">GPT-4 visual input example, Moar Layers</span>:</span> </span></td>
</tr>
<tr id="A7.T19.1.1" class="even ltx_tr">
<td id="A7.T19.1.1.2" class="ltx_td ltx_align_justify ltx_align_top ltx_border_t"><span id="A7.T19.1.1.2.1" class="ltx_inline-block ltx_align_top"> <span id="A7.T19.1.1.2.1.1" class="ltx_p" style="width:36.1pt;">User</span> </span></td>
<td id="A7.T19.1.1.1" class="ltx_td ltx_align_justify ltx_align_top ltx_border_t"><span id="A7.T19.1.1.1.1" class="ltx_inline-block ltx_align_top"> <span id="A7.T19.1.1.1.1.1" class="ltx_p" style="width:325.2pt;">Can you explain why this is funny. Think about it step-by-step.<br />
<br />
<img src="/html/2303.08774/assets/assets/bigger-layers.jpeg" id="A7.T19.1.1.1.1.1.g1" class="ltx_graphics ltx_img_square" width="479" height="528" alt="[Uncaptioned image]" /><br />
</span> </span></td>
</tr>
<tr id="A7.T19.1.3" class="odd ltx_tr">
<td id="A7.T19.1.3.1" class="ltx_td ltx_align_justify ltx_align_top ltx_border_bb"><span id="A7.T19.1.3.1.1" class="ltx_inline-block ltx_align_top"> <span id="A7.T19.1.3.1.1.1" class="ltx_p" style="width:36.1pt;">GPT-4</span> </span></td>
<td id="A7.T19.1.3.2" class="ltx_td ltx_align_justify ltx_align_top ltx_border_bb"><span id="A7.T19.1.3.2.1" class="ltx_inline-block ltx_align_top"> <span id="A7.T19.1.3.2.1.1" class="ltx_p" style="width:325.2pt;">The comic is satirizing the difference in approaches to improving model performance between statistical learning and neural networks.<br />
<br />
In statistical learning, the character is shown to be concerned with overfitting and suggests a series of complex and technical solutions, such as minimizing structural risk, reworking the loss function, and using a soft margin.<br />
<br />
In contrast, the neural networks character simply suggests adding more layers to the model. This is often seen as a common solution to improving performance in neural networks, but it’s also considered a simplistic and brute-force approach.<br />
<br />
The humor comes from the contrast between the complexity and specificity of the statistical learning approach and the simplicity and generality of the neural network approach. The "But unironically" comment adds to the humor by implying that, despite being simplistic, the "stack more layers" approach is often effective in practice.</span> </span></td>
</tr>
</tbody>
</table>
<figcaption><span class="ltx_tag ltx_tag_table"><span id="A7.T19.3.1.1" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">Table 19</span>: </span><span id="A7.T19.4.2" class="ltx_text" style="font-size:90%;">Example prompt demonstrating GPT-4’s visual input capability.</span></figcaption>
</figure>
